NodePort scan CLEAN in both regions — 0 nodePorts across 181 region-A and 157 region-B Services. **`secondaryDegraded: true` on this record is a COUNTING ARTIFACT, not a region-B outage**: region A reports hrReady 65/65 `degraded: false`, region B 57/65 `degraded: true`, but every one of region B's non-Ready HelmReleases (`bp-catalyst-platform`, `bp-continuum`, `bp-openova-mcp`, `bp-self-sovereign-cutover`, plus the Hetzner-only `bp-hcloud-ccm` / `bp-cluster-autoscaler-hcloud` / `bp-velero`) carries `suspend: true`, which `clusters/_template/bootstrap-kit/13-bp-catalyst-platform.yaml:47-54` declares BY DESIGN — `SECONDARY_HR_SUSPEND` renders `true` on every secondary CP because the Sovereign control plane belongs in the primary region. Region A symmetrically suspends `bp-catalyst-secondary-edge`, which is Ready in region B. The readiness counter scores intentionally-suspended releases as un-ready, so a 2-region prov can never reach parity and always reports its secondary degraded. The cutover has NOT been fired here — `self-sovereign-cutover-status` reads `cutoverComplete=false`, `progressPercent=0`, all 44 per-step keys empty — so every cutover-dependent row is honestly unreachable rather than red; no customer Organization exists, so the funnel-terminal, per-Org and cross-Org rows are unreachable for the same structural reason rather than a defect.)

> Measured 2026-08-11: **33 of 40** red rows established "the running artifact"

> all. For every console-rendered clause — the whole topology block, W1, W2 —

> 2026-08-02 build (`fad88bd`) for eight days behind a rollout deadlock while
> `catalyst-api` rolled six times in one night. Those stamps never checked
> whether the fix under test was in the bundle being rendered.
>
> * A **console/UI** clause is verified against the **served bundle** — fetch the
>   asset and grep it for the fix's marker. Not the API version, not `git log`.
> * An **API/controller** clause is verified against that component's image.
> * **Never cite an image sha as a row's evidence.** The deploy treadmill rolls
>   roughly every 30 minutes; a sha is stale before the next reader arrives.
>   Cite what you observed on the rendered surface.

## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | ⏳ | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING — CARRIED | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when the env is replaced: the row held on a PREDECESSOR env, so its verdict is carried and awaits re-confirmation here — distinct from never-walked (`☐`). **Nothing is deleted.** The Evidence cell keeps the original stamp verbatim, env label and artifact link included, `docs/ledger/uat-observations.csv` is untouched, and `scripts/uat-confidence.py` decays the row's confidence rather than zeroing it (founder 2026-08-11 — a wipe is not a failure). What is withdrawn is only the claim that a wiped machine's screenshot proves something about this one, which is the claim nobody made. A row may not be REPORTED in this state. | Re-walking on the live env — `uat-confidence.py --due` says which rows this cycle owes — then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env.** A ✅ answers one question — did this hold ON THE CURRENT
ENV — so `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` refuses a ✅ row that cites a wiped env's
artifact. A drop across an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one
env is a regression.

That is NOT a flush. The rule was once written "each new env flushes ALL prior
evidence", and under it the score reset to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured:
133 green 2026-07-30 → 25 on 2026-08-03) — which is the treadmill
`scripts/uat-confidence.py` exists to end. Founder 2026-08-11 superseded it: *"That
is the whole point not to reset the fucking success results to zero!!!"* A wipe is
not a failure. Since then `scripts/reset-uat.py` CARRIES each such row forward as
`⏳` with its evidence intact, and the scheduler decays its confidence instead of
deleting it, so a fresh Sovereign owes ~72 rows of real attention rather than 286.
The two rules met nowhere for a while, and on 2026-08-13 that gap put `main` red:
the guard demanded a re-mark, the reset was a no-op, and the guard's own remediation
pointed at it. They now meet inside `reset-uat.py`, which asserts the guard is
satisfied before it writes.

**Env:** live omantel.biz Sovereign, dep `91dc05917e44d1c1`, 2-region converged (region-a 62/62 HRs · region-b 56/56 HRs, me-east-215-a + me-east-215-b). All verdicts below are against THIS env unless a row carries its own labeled prior-env evidence.

**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

Re-walk scope: scripts/uat-confidence.py --due. ‖ hw295-2026-08-13T02:2xZ LIVE DATAPATH MEASUREMENT, BOTH REGIONS, WITH A FAILING CONTROL. ✅ — this row asks for two different things and the earlier ☐ was correct to separate them: a CONFIG read is not a DATAPATH proof. Both are now done. CONFIG, read per-NODE (not from the ConfigMap) via the hostNetwork cilium agent, and IDENTICAL in both regions: eth0=**1500**, cilium_host=**1420**, cilium_net=**1420**, cilium_wg0=**1340** — exactly the three figures this clause names for routing-mode=native since #4656. DATAPATH, measured with DF actually set: from a pod, a UDP socket with `IP_MTU_DISCOVER=IP_PMTUDISC_DO` connected to a pod on a DIFFERENT node reports pathMTU=**1340**, and a send of exactly pathMTU-28 = 1312 bytes SUCCEEDS. Measured cross-node inside region A (10.42.1.150, 10.42.3.86), cross-node inside region B (10.43.1.30, 10.43.4.154), and CROSS-REGION in both directions (A->10.43.1.30, B->10.42.1.150). Every path reports the same 1340 — no black-holed or silently-reduced PMTU anywhere. THE CONTROL, which is what makes the above mean anything: one byte more (1313) is REFUSED with `EMSGSIZE / Message too long` on every single path. The probe can fail, and does, at exactly the predicted boundary. WHY 1340 AND NOT 1420 — internally consistent, not a discrepancy: `enable-wireguard=true`, so all pod-to-pod traffic is encrypted through cilium_wg0, whose MTU IS 1340. 1420 is the unencrypted pod/cilium_host figure and 1500 the node figure; the effective pod path MTU is correctly the wg0 one. DELIVERY, so this does not rest on a send() return alone: the DF probe proves the local stack's PMTU and DF handling, while the CNPG WAL stream proves bytes actually arrive — every standby reads `state=streaming` at a matching LSN across nodes AND across regions, which is sustained bulk TCP over exactly these paths. |

| 29 | sso | [#3374](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3374) | Re-open the bare console URL after the session TTL → lands signed-in again, no PIN re-prompt. | hw295-2026-08-12 | ☐ | hw295-2026-08-12T15:26Z — ATTEMPTED AND INVALIDATED BY MY OWN PROBE; recorded so the next walker does not repeat it. I cleared the browser context's cookies (114 -> 0) and re-opened `https://console.hw295.omani.works/`. It landed on `/login` — `Sign in / Enter your email to receive a 6-digit PIN` — with `whoami` returning 401. THAT IS NOT THIS ROW'S SCENARIO AND IS NOT A DEFECT. The clause is re-entry AFTER THE CONSOLE SESSION'S TTL, where the console's own cookie is gone but the KEYCLOAK SSO session survives and re-authenticates silently. Clearing ALL cookies destroyed the Keycloak session too, which makes the browser a brand-new identity — and a brand-new browser being asked for a PIN is exactly right. Stamping ❌ on this would have libelled correct behaviour. THE CORRECT PROBE, for whoever runs it: clear ONLY cookies scoped to `console.<fqdn>` (leave the IdP host's cookies intact), then re-open the bare console URL and assert it lands on /dashboard with no PIN prompt. COST, stated plainly: this over-clear also ended the authenticated session this walk had been using, so no further session-walled row could be measured afterwards. Re-entry needs a fresh PIN, and the PIN is mailed to the founder's personal mailbox, which is read-only-off-limits for walk automation (a prior agent rotated its password and locked the founder out of email). The sanctioned alternative is minting the session via the handover signer. METHOD FINDING 2026-08-13T04:0xZ — an HTTP-level probe CANNOT decide this row, and recording that so the next walker does not bank a false pass. `GET https://console.<fqdn>/` returns **200 with an owner session AND 200 with no session at all**, and the two responses are **byte-identical** (md5 equal, 1063 bytes, `<title>OpenOva Corporate</title>`, body is just `<div id="root"></div>`). The console is an SPA shell: the auth decision is made client-side after the bundle loads, so the status code carries no authentication signal whatsoever. A 200 here is the classic surface-that-cannot-fail — it would have let me stamp this row green on evidence that an ANONYMOUS request reproduces exactly. This row therefore requires a real browser that executes the bundle and observes whether it settles on the dashboard or bounces to the PIN prompt. SECOND CONSTRAINT, measured: the live `catalyst_session_hint` cookie carries `expires=1786601821` against a walk-time `now=1786593689` — about 2.3 hours of TTL REMAINING — so the clause's literal *after the session TTL* precondition is not satisfiable on this session yet; it needs an aged or deliberately-expired session, not merely a re-open. |

This row is no longer 'unwalked'; it is walked and failing for a named reason. MEASURED with an authenticated owner session: `POST /api/v1/sovereigns/<dep>/applications/preview` with `{blueprintRef: bp-wordpress@0.4.23, organizationRef: hw295-omani-works, placement:{mode:"active-hot-standby", regions:[me-east-215-a, me-east-215-b]}}` returns **HTTP 200** and FOUR manifests — a kustomization + helmrelease per region, so the 2-REGION half of the clause is satisfied. 🛑 BUT BOTH REGIONS RENDER AS PRIMARY: region-a reads `# Region: me-east-215-a (role=primary)` / `catalyst.openova.io/placement-role: primary`, and region-b reads the SAME. A scan of the whole payload finds `role=standby` FALSE, `standbyType` FALSE, `role=replica` FALSE. So there is no standby leg, and therefore no 'region-a primary + region-b replica' pair and nothing for a Switchover to arm against. The Application CR's `spec.placement.mode` is correct (`active-hot-standby`) — only the per-region role is wrong. ROOT CAUSE, at source: `applications_preview.go` compared the RAW mode against the LEGACY spelling `"active-hotstandby"` in three places (the `previewRoleForPlacement` switch + two `standby :=` sites). The CANONICAL token is `active-hot-standby` — what `validPlacementMode` accepts and names in its own error text, and 112 of the repo's 141 mode literals. Sent canonically it fell through to `return "primary"`. The alias mapper `canonicalizeTopology` already existed in the same package and was simply not called. FAILURE MODE WORTH NAMING: 200, no error, no warning — `placement-role` is what the topology surface and DR wiring read, so an operator choosing hot-standby would get two primaries and nothing would say so. TO CLOSE: merge #6201, roll catalyst-api, re-run this preview and confirm region-b renders `role=standby`; then install and check the app's Topology tab shows the pair with Switchover armed. NOT claimed by the fix: the RECONCILE path (`application_controller.go:1359` sets PlacementRole from `rp.Role`) is a different source and is unproven for the canonical spelling. RE-WALKED 2026-08-13T04:0xZ on the RUNNING image `catalyst-api:1240b46`, which provably carries the fix (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 0227e6d40 1240b4600` = true). **FIX MERGED on main as 0227e6d40 (PR #6201)** and the role defect is GONE, measured not asserted: `POST /api/v1/sovereigns/952c564cab38d320/applications/preview` with `placement.mode=active-hot-standby` returned 200 and rendered **region me-east-215-a = `primary`, region me-east-215-b = `standby`** across 4 manifests. Before #6201 the same call rendered TWO primaries. WHAT STILL FAILS, now precisely isolated: the preview emits only `kustomization.yaml` + `helmrelease.yaml` per region and **no Continuum CR**, so the clause's *armed Switchover* half has no producer at this layer — the same missing-Continuum-CR shape as #4986. The row therefore stays ❌ on the Switchover half alone, not on placement roles. The provision-and-read-the-Topology-tab half was deliberately NOT run here: creating an Application mid-cutover would put a fresh HelmRelease mid-roll and perturb the settled-roll pre-flight this env is currently exercising. CORRECTION, same session: the sentence above that the Switchover half "has no producer at this layer" measured the WRONG COMPONENT and is withdrawn. The per-app Continuum producer exists and is a full reconcile path in the **application-controller** — `core/controllers/application/internal/controller/continuum.go` (`buildContinuumPlan` -> `renderContinuumCR` -> `reconcileContinuumCR` upserts `Continuum.dr.openova.io`). It is spelling-safe by construction (it gates on the typed enum `BcpActiveHotStandby`, whose value IS the canonical `active-hot-standby`, reached via the enum-to-enum `patternToBcpTopology`), so it does NOT carry #6200's defect. It simply never appears in a PREVIEW, because the preview renders the GitOps commit (kustomization + helmrelease) while the Continuum CR is written at reconcile. The Blueprint gate is also satisfied for the app I probed: `platform/wordpress-tenant/blueprint.yaml` declares `switchover: {mechanism: bp-continuum, rtoSeconds: 60, rpoSeconds: 0}`. So the honest status of this row is: the placement-role defect is FIXED and measured, and the Switchover half is UNPROVEN-ON-THIS-ENV rather than absent — it needs a real provision plus a Topology-tab read, deliberately deferred until the in-flight cutover reaches cutoverComplete so a fresh HelmRelease does not perturb the settled-roll pre-flight. |
